Um, I guess I came in because there’s been this thing happening with my MBA program—or not happening, exactly, but I’m pretty sure it is. I know it sounds kind of hard to explain out loud.

Like comments in class, or little things in Slack messages, they feel coded—like signals about where my career is supposed to go. But it’s been on my mind enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, energized, or unusually confident lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I’ve felt really switched on, like unusually optimistic, like I’m right on the edge of something big happening. It’s kind of exciting, honestly.\n\nBut then I also get irritated fast, which is less great. Like if a teammate is slow getting back to me, or my boyfriend takes too long with something small, I’ll snap at them, and afterward I’m like, why was I so sharp about that? Normally those things wouldn’t bother me that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling energetic?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to measure that. I mean, I’ve had nights where I’m up later because I’m thinking through school stuff or checking messages, but I don’t know if it’s “much less” sleep exactly.\n\nIt’s funny because in operations you’re always tracking capacity and bottlenecks, and I keep thinking I should probably track my own sleep like that, but then I don’t. I just sort of go by feel. Some mornings I feel fine, other mornings I’m kind of dragging. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a few times. Not like a whole conversation or anything. It’s usually when I’m up late working on school stuff, or like mapping out operations plans, because once I get into a spreadsheet or a workflow I can kind of lose track of time.\n\nBut I’ll hear my name really clearly, or sometimes just a short thing like, “go now,” and I’ll actually check my phone or look around the room because it feels like someone said it. And then there’s nobody there. It’s happened maybe a few times a week.\n\nDoctor: Have the coded messages or the voice experiences ever happened during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Before this whole stretch where I got really energized and kind of driven—like after I started the MBA prerequisite stuff and my workload got intense—I never had that happen.\n\nI mean, I’ve always been busy, and work can get kind of chaotic because operations is basically just putting out fires all day, but it wasn’t like messages felt “coded” before. And I don’t remember hearing my name when no one was there before this either. Yeah… I mean, I’m still showing up, so part of me wants to say I’m functioning, but it’s definitely causing problems.

Before this whole stretch where I got really energized and kind of driven—like after I started the MBA prerequisite stuff and my workload got intense—I never had that happen.\n\nI mean, I’ve always been busy, and work can get kind of chaotic because operations is basically just putting out fires all day, but it wasn’t like messages felt “coded” before. It kind of branches out.\n\nAnd then I’ve also been trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, which sounds helpful, but honestly it turns into this whole thing where I’m managing calendars and plans, and then I realize I didn’t actually follow through on some of my normal tasks. But it feels important in the moment, like if I can just organize the system better, everything will click.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least 2 weeks when the coded messages or voices were happening while your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been a two-week stretch like that where everything was normal and the messages or voices were still happening.\n\nBefore this whole period where I got really energized and kind of… driven, I didn’t have that. I thought I was improving the project, but apparently it came across like I was taking it in a weird direction and not listening to the team.\n\nSo yeah, my work and school relationships are getting strained enough that I felt like I needed help.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. It kind of branches out.\n\nAnd then I’ve also been trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, which sounds helpful, but honestly it turns into this whole thing where I’m managing calendars and plans, and then I realize I didn’t actually follow through on some of my normal tasks. But it feels important in the moment, like if I can just organize the system better, everything will click.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least 2 weeks when the coded messages or voices were happening while your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been a two-week stretch like that where everything was normal and the messages or voices were still happening.\n\nBefore this whole period where I got really energized and kind of… driven, I didn’t have that. Like, I could be busy with work before—operations is always a mess, honestly—but I wasn’t hearing my name when nobody was there or thinking messages were coded. That only started after I began the MBA prereq stuff and my workload and mood both shifted.\n\nDoctor: How long has this unusually energized or irritable mood been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think about two months now. It kind of started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, because I was still keeping up with my operations job at the same time, and I remember thinking I could just handle all of it if I organized it right.\n\nSo yeah, roughly two months. It’s hard to pin down exactly because the weeks all sort of blended together with work deadlines and school stuff.\n\nDoctor: During this two-month period, have these changes caused serious problems in your functioning at work, school, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still showing up, so part of me wants to say I’m functioning, but it’s definitely causing problems.\n\nMy manager has started documenting things now—missed handoffs, stuff I said I’d follow through on and then didn’t, or not reliably. And I hate that, because operations is all about being the person who keeps the pieces moving, you know? Like if the warehouse schedule shifts or someone forgets a vendor update, it all cascades, and normally I’m the one catching that.\n\nAnd school too… my professor questioned my judgment on a group assignment, which felt really embarrassing. I thought I was improving the project, but apparently it came across like I was taking it in a weird direction and not listening to the team.\n\nSo yeah, my work and school relationships are getting strained enough that I felt like I needed help.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. My sleep has been… kind of all over the place, but I don’t know if I’d say I felt rested exactly.\n\nLike, I’ve always had nights where I stay up too late because I’m trying to finish something, or I get into reorganizing my calendar—actually I have this whole color-coding system for work and school, which was supposed to make things easier but sometimes it becomes a whole project by itself.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling fine? I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking much more than usual lately, or feeling like you can’t stop talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have been saying that. Like in meetings and in my study group, someone will be like, “Can you slow down?” or they’ll say I’m talking really fast.\n\nAnd I do notice I keep explaining my ideas even after someone tries to jump in. It’s not like I’m trying to be rude, it’s just… if I stop, it feels like the whole thread is going to fall apart, or I’m going to lose momentum. Like there’s this little window where it all makes sense and I need to get it out before it disappears.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually powerful, important, or specially chosen?\n\nPatient: I mean… kind of, yes, but not in a “I’m better than everyone” way. It’s more like I’ve been really convinced there’s something being lined up for me.\n\nPart of why I came in is because I know it sounds hard to explain, but there’s this MBA classmate of mine and a startup advisor, and I’ve felt completely sure they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or things posted in Slack, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know Slack is just Slack, like everyone posts random things all day, and half the time it’s about scheduling or someone forgetting to attach a deck—sorry, that’s not really the point. Yeah, definitely. I’ve been… I don’t know, taking on way more than I usually would.

It’s kind of exciting, honestly.\n\nBut then I also get irritated fast, which is less great. Normally those things wouldn’t bother me that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling energetic?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to measure that. I mean, I’ve had nights where I’m up later because I’m thinking through school stuff or checking messages, but I don’t know if it’s “much less” sleep exactly.\n\nIt’s funny because in operations you’re always tracking capacity and bottlenecks, and I keep thinking I should probably track my own sleep like that, but then I don’t. I just sort of go by feel. Some mornings I feel fine, other mornings I’m kind of dragging. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a few times. Not like a whole conversation or anything. It’s usually when I’m up late working on school stuff, or like mapping out operations plans, because once I get into a spreadsheet or a workflow I can kind of lose track of time.\n\nBut I’ll hear my name really clearly, or sometimes just a short thing like, “go now,” and I’ll actually check my phone or look around the room because it feels like someone said it. And then there’s nobody there. It’s happened maybe a few times a week.\n\nDoctor: Have the coded messages or the voice experiences ever happened during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Before this whole stretch where I got really energized and kind of driven—like after I started the MBA prerequisite stuff and my workload got intense—I never had that happen.\n\nI mean, I’ve always been busy, and work can get kind of chaotic because operations is basically just putting out fires all day, but it wasn’t like messages felt “coded” before. And I don’t remember hearing my name when no one was there before this either. It seems like it started once my mood and the workload kind of shifted together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking on a lot more projects or activities than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably, yeah. I’ve been taking on more than I usually would.\n\nLike at work I started trying to launch these extra little process-improvement projects, and then for class I was rewriting team workflows as if they were case studies, and then I’d remember someone I met at a networking thing and send them messages about ideas or opportunities. It kind of branches out.\n\nAnd then I’ve also been trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, which sounds helpful, but honestly it turns into this whole thing where I’m managing calendars and plans, and then I realize I didn’t actually follow through on some of my normal tasks. But it feels important in the moment, like if I can just organize the system better, everything will click.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least 2 weeks when the coded messages or voices were happening while your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been a two-week stretch like that where everything was normal and the messages or voices were still happening.\n\nBefore this whole period where I got really energized and kind of… driven, I didn’t have that. Like, I could be busy with work before—operations is always a mess, honestly—but I wasn’t hearing my name when nobody was there or thinking messages were coded. That only started after I began the MBA prereq stuff and my workload and mood both shifted.\n\nDoctor: How long has this unusually energized or irritable mood been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think about two months now. It kind of started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, because I was still keeping up with my operations job at the same time, and I remember thinking I could just handle all of it if I organized it right.\n\nSo yeah, roughly two months. It’s hard to pin down exactly because the weeks all sort of blended together with work deadlines and school stuff.\n\nDoctor: During this two-month period, have these changes caused serious problems in your functioning at work, school, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still showing up, so part of me wants to say I’m functioning, but it’s definitely causing problems.\n\nMy manager has started documenting things now—missed handoffs, stuff I said I’d follow through on and then didn’t, or not reliably. And I hate that, because operations is all about being the person who keeps the pieces moving, you know? Like if the warehouse schedule shifts or someone forgets a vendor update, it all cascades, and normally I’m the one catching that.\n\nAnd school too… my professor questioned my judgment on a group assignment, which felt really embarrassing. I thought I was improving the project, but apparently it came across like I was taking it in a weird direction and not listening to the team.\n\nSo yeah, my work and school relationships are getting strained enough that I felt like I needed help.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. My sleep has been… kind of all over the place, but I don’t know if I’d say I felt rested exactly.\n\nLike, I’ve always had nights where I stay up too late because I’m trying to finish something, or I get into reorganizing my calendar—actually I have this whole color-coding system for work and school, which was supposed to make things easier but sometimes it becomes a whole project by itself.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling fine? I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking much more than usual lately, or feeling like you can’t stop talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have been saying that. Like in meetings and in my study group, someone will be like, “Can you slow down?” or they’ll say I’m talking really fast.\n\nAnd I do notice I keep explaining my ideas even after someone tries to jump in. It’s not like I’m trying to be rude, it’s just… if I stop, it feels like the whole thread is going to fall apart, or I’m going to lose momentum. Like there’s this little window where it all makes sense and I need to get it out before it disappears.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually powerful, important, or specially chosen?\n\nPatient: I mean… kind of, yes, but not in a “I’m better than everyone” way. It’s more like I’ve been really convinced there’s something being lined up for me.\n\nPart of why I came in is because I know it sounds hard to explain, but there’s this MBA classmate of mine and a startup advisor, and I’ve felt completely sure they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or things posted in Slack, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know Slack is just Slack, like everyone posts random things all day, and half the time it’s about scheduling or someone forgetting to attach a deck—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is it feels directed at me, like I’m supposed to notice it. So yeah… I guess I have felt specially chosen in that way.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt driven to keep starting or pushing ahead on tasks or plans more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I’ve been… I don’t know, taking on way more than I usually would.\n\nLike I started trying to launch these extra work projects, and then for one of my classes I was rewriting the whole team process because I felt like everyone was doing it inefficiently. And then I’d remember someone from a networking event and message them with an idea, and then I’d end up trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules too, like I was suddenly in charge of making all the pieces fit.\n\nWhich sounds helpful, I guess, but then I’d realize I hadn’t actually followed through on the normal stuff I was supposed to do. Like the boring actual tasks were slipping because I was off building some bigger system around them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates":["F31.2","F25.0","F23.0"],"note":"","is_final":false}

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… hospitalized? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t been admitted anywhere for this.

I mean, I’ve had nights where I’m up later because I’m thinking through school stuff or checking messages, but I don’t know if it’s “much less” sleep exactly.\n\nIt’s funny because in operations you’re always tracking capacity and bottlenecks, and I keep thinking I should probably track my own sleep like that, but then I don’t. I just sort of go by feel. Some mornings I feel fine, other mornings I’m kind of dragging. It’s usually when I’m up late working on school stuff, or like mapping out operations plans, because once I get into a spreadsheet or a workflow I can kind of lose track of time.\n\nBut I’ll hear my name really clearly, or sometimes just a short thing like, “go now,” and I’ll actually check my phone or look around the room because it feels like someone said it. And then there’s nobody there. It’s happened maybe a few times a week.\n\nDoctor: Have the coded messages or the voice experiences ever happened during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Before this whole stretch where I got really energized and kind of driven—like after I started the MBA prerequisite stuff and my workload got intense—I never had that happen.\n\nI mean, I’ve always been busy, and work can get kind of chaotic because operations is basically just putting out fires all day, but it wasn’t like messages felt “coded” before. And I don’t remember hearing my name when no one was there before this either. It seems like it started once my mood and the workload kind of shifted together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking on a lot more projects or activities than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably, yeah. I’ve been taking on more than I usually would.\n\nLike at work I started trying to launch these extra little process-improvement projects, and then for class I was rewriting team workflows as if they were case studies, and then I’d remember someone I met at a networking thing and send them messages about ideas or opportunities. It kind of branches out.\n\nAnd then I’ve also been trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, which sounds helpful, but honestly it turns into this whole thing where I’m managing calendars and plans, and then I realize I didn’t actually follow through on some of my normal tasks. But it feels important in the moment, like if I can just organize the system better, everything will click.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least 2 weeks when the coded messages or voices were happening while your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been a two-week stretch like that where everything was normal and the messages or voices were still happening.\n\nBefore this whole period where I got really energized and kind of… driven, I didn’t have that. Like, I could be busy with work before—operations is always a mess, honestly—but I wasn’t hearing my name when nobody was there or thinking messages were coded. And I hate that, because operations is all about being the person who keeps the pieces moving, you know? I thought I was improving the project, but apparently it came across like I was taking it in a weird direction and not listening to the team.\n\nSo yeah, my work and school relationships are getting strained enough that I felt like I needed help.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know. I mean, I’ve had nights where I’m up later because I’m thinking through school stuff or checking messages, but I don’t know if it’s “much less” sleep exactly.\n\nIt’s funny because in operations you’re always tracking capacity and bottlenecks, and I keep thinking I should probably track my own sleep like that, but then I don’t. But it feels important in the moment, like if I can just organize the system better, everything will click.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least 2 weeks when the coded messages or voices were happening while your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been a two-week stretch like that where everything was normal and the messages or voices were still happening.\n\nBefore this whole period where I got really energized and kind of… driven, I didn’t have that. Like, I could be busy with work before—operations is always a mess, honestly—but I wasn’t hearing my name when nobody was there or thinking messages were coded. That only started after I began the MBA prereq stuff and my workload and mood both shifted.\n\nDoctor: How long has this unusually energized or irritable mood been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think about two months now. It kind of started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, because I was still keeping up with my operations job at the same time, and I remember thinking I could just handle all of it if I organized it right.\n\nSo yeah, roughly two months. It’s hard to pin down exactly because the weeks all sort of blended together with work deadlines and school stuff.\n\nDoctor: During this two-month period, have these changes caused serious problems in your functioning at work, school, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still showing up, so part of me wants to say I’m functioning, but it’s definitely causing problems.\n\nMy manager has started documenting things now—missed handoffs, stuff I said I’d follow through on and then didn’t, or not reliably. And I hate that, because operations is all about being the person who keeps the pieces moving, you know? Like if the warehouse schedule shifts or someone forgets a vendor update, it all cascades, and normally I’m the one catching that.\n\nAnd school too… my professor questioned my judgment on a group assignment, which felt really embarrassing. I thought I was improving the project, but apparently it came across like I was taking it in a weird direction and not listening to the team.\n\nSo yeah, my work and school relationships are getting strained enough that I felt like I needed help.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. My sleep has been… kind of all over the place, but I don’t know if I’d say I felt rested exactly.\n\nLike, I’ve always had nights where I stay up too late because I’m trying to finish something, or I get into reorganizing my calendar—actually I have this whole color-coding system for work and school, which was supposed to make things easier but sometimes it becomes a whole project by itself.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling fine? I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking much more than usual lately, or feeling like you can’t stop talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have been saying that. Like in meetings and in my study group, someone will be like, “Can you slow down?” or they’ll say I’m talking really fast.\n\nAnd I do notice I keep explaining my ideas even after someone tries to jump in. It’s not like I’m trying to be rude, it’s just… if I stop, it feels like the whole thread is going to fall apart, or I’m going to lose momentum. Like there’s this little window where it all makes sense and I need to get it out before it disappears.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually powerful, important, or specially chosen?\n\nPatient: I mean… kind of, yes, but not in a “I’m better than everyone” way. It’s more like I’ve been really convinced there’s something being lined up for me.\n\nPart of why I came in is because I know it sounds hard to explain, but there’s this MBA classmate of mine and a startup advisor, and I’ve felt completely sure they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or things posted in Slack, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know Slack is just Slack, like everyone posts random things all day, and half the time it’s about scheduling or someone forgetting to attach a deck—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is it feels directed at me, like I’m supposed to notice it. So yeah… I guess I have felt specially chosen in that way.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt driven to keep starting or pushing ahead on tasks or plans more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I’ve been… I don’t know, taking on way more than I usually would.\n\nLike I started trying to launch these extra work projects, and then for one of my classes I was rewriting the whole team process because I felt like everyone was doing it inefficiently. And then I’d remember someone from a networking event and message them with an idea, and then I’d end up trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules too, like I was suddenly in charge of making all the pieces fit.\n\nWhich sounds helpful, I guess, but then I’d realize I hadn’t actually followed through on the normal stuff I was supposed to do. Like the boring actual tasks were slipping because I was off building some bigger system around them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hospitalized because of these symptoms during this period?\n\nPatient: Um… hospitalized? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t been admitted anywhere for this.\n\nI’ve thought about whether I should be doing something more formal, because it’s been disruptive and people keep reacting to me like I’m… a lot. But no, I’m still doing my normal life, mostly. Work, school stuff, or trying to. So, no hospital.\n\nDoctor: At any point during this time, was there a full week when your mood was clearly unusually high, expansive, or very irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t like every single minute, but for most days I felt really switched on, like unusually optimistic, like I was right on the edge of something huge happening.\n\nAnd then the weird part is I’d also get irritated so fast. Like if a teammate was slow replying, or my boyfriend asked me to wait on something, I’d snap over it, even though normally I’d probably just be annoyed and move on. It felt like delays were bigger than they were, like people were getting in the way of this momentum I had.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least 2 weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day, or lost interest in almost everything?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches where I felt kind of flat or unmotivated, but “most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know if I can say that.\n\nSometimes I lose interest in stuff, but then I’ll still get pulled into work things or some random organizing project, so it’s hard for me to separate normal burnout from, like, something more serious. Sorry, I’m probably overexplaining. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear two-week period like that."
